# nana-pi requirements

The standing, numbered requirement set for the toolkit itself: the pi extension pack, the knowledge pull, the installer, the bench harness and the repo test runner. nana-pi ships the requirements-first pattern to every project it scaffolds or adopts; this file is the toolkit living under its own rules.

Rows are EARS form (one `shall` sentence per row from the Ubiquitous, WHEN, WHILE, IF-THEN and WHERE templates) and spec the contract, not the design; a row that is not `retired` carries exactly one `shall` outside a code span (G-013). IDs are stable and never renumbered or reused; nana-pi collapses a removed R-row in place as `retired`, keeping its ID and a snapshot pointer to the full row; a split keeps the original ID for the first clause. Cells contain no `|`.

Statuses: `implemented` (a test in this repo asserts it; cited as `<package or app>/tests/<file>::<test title>`, backticked), `untested` (the code does it, nothing pins it), `planned`, `violated` (the code contradicts a documented rule; the cell says where), `retired`. Evidence in a sibling repo carries that repo as a prefix and is exempt from the local rail.

Trace: `npm test` fails when a status disagrees with the suite. A test declares the rows it evidences with a `// req: R-001` comment directly above its `test(` / `check(` call. An `implemented` row with no marker fails; an `untested`, `planned` or `violated` row that a marker traces fails; a marker naming an unknown id fails; a cited test must exist and carry the marker. The rail also reports the count of rows off EARS form on its own `ears:` line every run and fails naming each off-form row once that count exceeds the declared allowance (G-014/G-015). `EARS_ALLOWANCE` in `scripts/requirements-trace.mjs` is 0 since 2026-10-04; the zero-off-form check remains (G-013). Sealed tunables: a contract number is defined once in the package's config surface with provenance naming its row and pinned by one test; a retune is a requirement diff first.

New behaviour in this repo: requirement diff, then tagged tests, then code.

## How this file is organized

The rows are grouped into a tree: capability → feature → subgroup. A capability heading reads
`# CAP-nn · <label>`, a feature heading `## FEAT-nnn · <label>` and a subgroup heading
`### SUB-nnn · <label>`. Each heading is followed by a `Purpose:` line saying why that part of the
product exists. A capability also carries an `Outcome:` line, and a feature a `Status:` line.
A capability may carry five optional lines in any order in its own span. `Kind:` is exactly
`product` or `constraint`; `Deliverables:` lists row IDs, `Needs:` and `Binds:` list capability
IDs, and `Retired:` lists successor capability IDs. A product capability may repeat `Made by:`
and `Read by:` lines for its deliverables, and the tree checker reports gaps in those lines as notes.
A feature under a kinded capability may carry `Artifact: A-nnn · <name> · <class>: <location> · <run>: <writer or reason>` lines after its lifecycle lines and before any subgroup. The six classes are `tracked`, `clone store`, `user-home store`, `other project`, `git ref` and `CI record`; a retired entry keeps its ID with `retired: <reason or successor>` in place of class and run. A feature records a cross-feature read as `Reads: A-nnn · <reader path>[, <reader path>]` after its artifact entries; each reader is a tracked file, and own reads stay implicit. A maker feature declares a reader of its own entry as `Read by: A-nnn · <kind>: <detail>`, where code and prompt name tracked files, person, rule and runtime give reasons, a test file is never a reader, and a currency or presence check is not a reader. Every live entry needs a declared reader or a hold written `Parked: A-nnn · since <YYYY-MM-DD>: <reason>`. A `Made by:` third part names one or more A-IDs directly, or says `built from:` before them.
The generated outline shows every field the checker reads after that capability's Parent line and each feature's artifact and read IDs after its Parent line; it leaves out artifact `Read by:` and `Parked:` lines.
Every row lives in exactly one table, its home: under one feature, or under one subgroup when its
feature is subdivided. A feature that also depends on rows homed elsewhere names them on its
`Also applies:` line. A row named there is shared, and a change to it has a wide blast radius.
Search this ledger for an ID to find its home and shares, or run `npm run req:tree` to write
an optional ignored local outline at `docs/requirements-tree.md`. The outline shows every heading
with its rows, each feature's status and shares, and a shared-row index, most listed first. Where the old numbered sections went is in
`docs/requirements-sections-2026-10-09.md`.

## How the tree is governed

Who decides. The owner decides every change to the tree: creating, renaming, moving, splitting,
merging, freezing, unfreezing or retiring a capability, feature or subgroup; moving a row to
another home; and adding or removing a share. The seat proposes these changes, and the change
declaration lists them; the next increment adds a field for them to the lane brief. A builder adds
or changes rows only in the feature its brief names.

Artifact entries and feature artifact read lines are added like rows by a builder within the feature its brief names. The movement report shows every entry, read, artifact `Read by:` and artifact `Parked:` change to the reviewer, the owner sees entries on the product page, and the owner may overrule them.

A feature's life. Its `Status:` reads `proposed` (agreed, no rows yet), `active`,
`frozen (<reason>)` or `retired (<successor or reason>)`. A frozen feature takes no discretionary
change to its rows or code; the ruling it names lists any exceptions. A proposed or retired
feature holds no rows. A retired feature keeps its heading and ID. Splitting or merging features
creates new IDs, and the old feature is retired naming its successors. Capability, feature and
subgroup IDs are never renumbered or reused.

A requirement's life. A new behaviour starts as a requirement diff in its home feature, then
tagged tests, then code. A new row names its shares in that diff. Before changing a row, search this ledger for its ID
(or write a local outline with `npm run req:tree` and use its shared index); a change to a shared row names its home and every feature that lists
it, and the review reads them all. A share is re-read in the same change when its row changes,
when its row moves home, and when either feature changes purpose or status, splits, merges or
retires; otherwise it stands until the next placement review. A frozen status is resolved in the
change that follows the ruling it names. Retiring an R-row removes it from every `Also applies:` line and collapses it in place as `| R-nnn | — | retired | <reason> (was <sha8>) |` in the
same change. A new retirement gives its reason in at most 120 characters, never bare `retired`; retain any "superseded by R-nnn" text. The pointer is the lane's base commit on main, which contains the full row, recoverable with `git show <sha8>:REQUIREMENTS.md`.
A split keeps the original ID for the first clause; a merge retires the merged rows with "superseded by <survivor>" in their Evidence. IDs are never renumbered or reused; retired A-entries keep their existing form.

What checks it. The suite checks the shape: R-287 to R-294 (homes, purposes, IDs, nesting, the
outline, shares, statuses, empty proposed and retired features), R-2440 to R-2447 (capability
kinds, fields, lift preservation, outline rendering and movement reporting), and R-2780 to R-2798 (artifact registry and read parsing, classes, binding, claim coverage, outline, movement and lift preservation). Review alone checks the rest:
who decided, that the declaration lists every tree change, that a shared row's change names every
lister, and that frozen features took no discretionary change.

## Purpose and outcomes

Purpose: a customized agentic coding experience that can be used for any development project,
and makes development more controlled, robust and effective (the owner, 2026-10-09). It serves the
objective in `~/nana-agent-loop/OBJECTIVE.md`: build products with agents, with nana-pi as the
shared toolkit and experience.

Outcomes (astra's 2026-10-09 draft, agreed by the owner):

1. **Understand what the product enables.** Find a capability, understand why it matters, and reach its relevant obligations without reading the whole ledger.
2. **Delegate one coherent change.** Distinguish the agreed increment from adjacent discoveries, blockers and future work.
3. **Accept a change with useful evidence.** See the behavior changed, the requirement delta, the candidate revision, the checks performed and the remaining uncertainty.
4. **Reuse the experience across projects.** Adopt the appropriate workflow and controls without an unrelated toolchain migration.
5. **Continue without reconstructing history.** Resume the current goal, scope, decisions and evidence with the right context available.

## FEAT-040 · Make and keep memory
Purpose: Turn past sessions and reviews into shaped, low-trust memory records that point back to their evidence, and keep them.

### SUB-023 · Keep a private owner profile

Purpose: Keep what the seat learns about its owner in a private, synced store, ranked by trust the owner's words earn.

| ID | Requirement | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-3230 | WHEN an enabled seat saves a quoted supported claim, the profile shall commit one shared-shape record with saved source, a shared user kind and seat evidence resolving to its quote, print its saved line, and refuse missing quotes, routed kinds and exceeded caps without writes. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/profile-store.test.mjs::a save writes one shape record into the private clone and nowhere else`; explicit save command coverage only; automatic seat saving is paused under R-4026. |
| R-3231 | The profile shall refuse writes from launcher roles, linked worktrees, disabled machines and missing pointers while accepting an enabled main checkout through equivalent git-directory spellings. | untested | Cut A 2026-11-09 removes the slow profile guard case; behavior stays. |
| R-3232 | The profile shall raise trust only through distinct-session distinct-quote evidence or a confirmation with new owner words. | untested | Cut A 2026-11-09 removes the slow profile guard case; behavior stays. |
| R-3233 | The profile shall derive retract and supersede liveness, resolve cycles by newest stamp, mark concurrent replacements and print bounded trust-ordered output. | untested | Cut A 2026-11-09 removes the slow profile guard case; behavior stays. |
| R-3234 | The profile shall preserve configuration members with a backup and initialise only a guarded private push target or an explicit unverified override. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/profile-store-guards.test.mjs::the pointer is configuration and the push target stays verified private` |
| R-3410 | IF `NANA_ROLE` is set, THEN every profile command shall refuse before reading the profile pointer or store. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/profile-store-guards.test.mjs::every profile command refuses under set launcher roles before reading the pointer` |
| R-3411 | WHEN a command retracts, confirms or supersedes a record, it shall resolve a unique case-insensitive hexadecimal prefix of at least 12 characters across profile and project records and write its event in that record's area. | untested | Cut A 2026-11-09 removes the slow profile guard case; behavior stays. |
| R-3235 | Two profile clones shall converge through append-only safe paths without merge conflicts or byte conversion. | untested | Cut A 2026-11-09 removes the slow sync tests; behavior stays. |
| R-3236 | The profile shall keep offline saves committed, serialize concurrent writers and recover stopped rebases before syncing. | untested | Cut A 2026-11-09 removes the slow sync tests; behavior stays. |
| R-3237 | The profile shall require 3 distinct sessions for medium trust. | untested | Coverage removed; behavior stays. diet1: removed the unearned seal. |
| R-3238 | The profile shall bound show output to 4000 characters. | untested | Coverage removed; behavior stays. diet1: removed the unearned seal. |
| R-3239 | The profile shall bound each git child to 15000 milliseconds. | untested | Coverage removed; behavior stays. diet1: removed the unearned seal. |
| R-3240 | The profile shall wait at most 420000 milliseconds for its clone lock, covering 28 Git steps at the 15000 millisecond Git timeout. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/profile-store.test.mjs::seal: PROFILE_LOCK_WAIT_MS covers 28 Git steps` |
| R-3241 | The profile shall consider a dead writer lock stale after 120000 milliseconds. | untested | Coverage removed; behavior stays. diet1: removed the unearned seal. |
| R-3242 | The profile shall retry a rejected push once. | untested | Coverage removed; behavior stays. diet1: removed the unearned seal. |
| R-3243 | The profile shall cap daily machine saves at 40, claims at 1200 characters and quotes at 4000 characters. | untested | Coverage removed; behavior stays. diet1: removed the unearned seal. |
| R-3244 | The profile shall select CLAUDE_SESSION_ID then PI_SESSION_ID then PI_SESSION_FILE before falling back to the local day. | untested | Coverage removed; behavior stays. diet1: removed the unearned seal. |
| R-3245 | The profile shall use lowercase colon-free UTC millisecond stamps. | untested | Coverage removed; behavior stays. diet1: removed the unearned seal. |
| R-3246 | The profile shall use lowercase safe paths of at most 180 characters and bounded git output. | untested | Coverage removed; behavior stays. diet1: removed the unearned seal. |
| R-3247 | The profile shall accept only the sealed GitHub HTTPS, SCP SSH and explicit SSH push URL forms for verification. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/profile-store.test.mjs::seal: PROFILE_REMOTE_FORMS` |
| R-3248 | The profile shall use the sealed records, events, marker and lock layout without text conversion. | untested | Coverage removed; behavior stays. diet1: removed the unearned seal. |

| R-3295 | The shared merge step shall keep one live normalized claim or unit and prefer the seat's saved record over extracted records. | untested | Profile compatibility remains in `packages/nana-pack/lib/profile-store.mjs`; the memory-run check was removed. |

## FEAT-005 · Communicate decisions clearly
Purpose: Deliver the preferred writing guidance and check report readability and structure.
Status: active
Also applies: R-641
Artifact: A-005 · writing rule · tracked: packages/nana-pack/rules/nana-writing.md · manual: the owner's adopted rule, edited by hand in reviewed lanes
Reads: A-011 · packages/nana-pack/lib/config.ts, packages/nana-pack/extensions/nana-writing.ts
Note (moved from old §13a on 2026-10-09):
G-001 and G-002 govern the tunables in `packages/nana-pack/lib/writing-config.mjs`; cited in that module's header, not duplicated here.
Note (moved from old §13b on 2026-10-09):
Amendment 1 (design-ruling.md, 2026-10-04, after astra r1 MUST 1): delivery moved from an
agent-dir `AGENTS.md` link (R-373 to R-375, deleted — never landed, IDs stay unused) to the
seventh pack extension, `extensions/nana-writing.ts`, which appends the rule the way
`nana-objective.ts` appends the objective.

A bounded read validates only the bytes it reads. Before decoding it trims a trailing sequence whose own lead byte proves it incomplete, never a byte invalid on its own (astra r3 MUST 1; the four UTF-8 tail tests).

| ID | Requirement | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-690 | WHERE --report is given and no first prose sentence exists, the checker shall report a missing verdict. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::verdict: empty input does NOT pass` |
| R-742 | WHEN no path is given, the writing checker shall read the text from stdin. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::stdin: a piped text is checked and named -` |
| R-743 | WHEN paths are given, the checker shall check each file and prefix each finding with the path and the line of the finding's first retained character, the verdict finding excepted (it carries line 1). | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::files: two files, findings carry path:line (different, non-1 lines)`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::line (CLI): a length finding starting after a soft-wrapped boundary gets line 2, not 1`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::line (CLI): a passive finding starting after a soft-wrapped boundary gets line 2, not 1 (astra r2 repro)` |
| R-744 | The checker shall report every sentence longer than SENTENCE_CAP words with its word count. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::length: a 26-word sentence is reported with its count`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::markdown: every over-cap sentence is reported, not just the first` |
| R-745 | The checker shall report every sentence that matches the passive-voice pattern and is not on the exception list, whole-word. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::passive: "was edited by" is reported`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::passive: "is indeed" exception is silent while "was written" (non-exception) is reported`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::passive: "is needed" (the genuine passive astra found missed) is now caught`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::passive: "is green" and "was wooden" (astra r1's false positives) are excepted` |
| R-746 | The checker shall report every occurrence of a banned word outside a code span, case-insensitive. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::banned: Dogfooding is reported once`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::banned: a banned word INSIDE a code span is not reported (a mention, not a use)`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::banned (CLI): every occurrence on one line is reported, not just the first` |
| R-747 | WHERE --report is given, the checker shall accept a listed uppercase verdict word or phrase only as the first token of the first prose sentence after optional emphasis markers. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::verdict: lowercase or later verdict words do not satisfy the first-token rule`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::verdict: bold uppercase first-token verdict passes`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::verdict: "YOUR CALL" matches as the two-word phrase it is` |
| R-748 | WHERE --report is given, the checker shall report every backtick span and every slash path, over prose blocks only — never a heading, a table row or a fence. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::report: a backtick span and a path are two findings`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::report (CLI): --report is honoured — identifier AND verdict lines both appear`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::report (CLI): without --report, no identifier or verdict line appears`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::markdown: identifiers in a heading, a table and a fence are never counted (prose only)` |
| R-749 | The checker shall end its output with one summary line, as its LAST and only such line, carrying the sentence count, the count of each check's findings, and (WHERE --report is given) the cross-file verdict share. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::summary: last line parses and the counts match`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::summary: one pass + one fail reads as verdict=1/2, not the first reading alone`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::summary: outside --report, verdict reads n/a`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-check.test.mjs::summary (CLI): the summary is the LAST line and appears exactly once` |

Rule text moved to packages/nana-pack/rules/nana-writing.md (Amendment 1 §A1). Amendment 1 §A3 overrules the earlier config-path wording: the Words bullet now names the banned word in a code span (the owner banned, then the word itself), since a pi session has no memory of a ban and will not open a file the rule merely points at. "A decision point" is now a numbered list (Amendment 1 §A2), ending the 27-word sentence astra r1 found escaping the cap through soft-wrap. Wording per Amendment 1 §A2 and §A3, ratified in land-ruling.md §2 |

# CAP-02 · Deliver controlled changes
Purpose: Give builders, reviewers and the integration step separate responsibilities and concrete records.
Outcome: A developer can accept a bounded change with understandable review evidence and a controlled integration step.
Kind: product

| R-1375 | WHEN the revision of a dirty or clean tree is derived in a repository with no clean or smudge filter, the derivation shall leave the repository's git folder with the same entries, each of the same size and mode, and run none of its hooks. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/review-ledger-state.test.mjs::snapshot derivation leaves git metadata unchanged`; reworded 2026-10-19 (clean-revision build 3, round 1 review) to drop the mtime clause: the scratch add -A re-hashes files against a stat-less index, and git "freshens" (touches the mtime of) an object it would write that already exists in the real store reached through the alternates file — content, size, mode and the entry set are unchanged; only mtime moves, and no git setting turns freshening off. |
| R-1376 | WHEN a file git checked out with CRLF endings under core.autocrlf is removed only from the index, the revision shall be the plain HEAD sha. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/review-ledger-state.test.mjs::cached removal of normalized content retains plain HEAD` |

### SUB-008 · Run and stop an isolated reviewer
Purpose: Keep reviewer roles separate, suppress unrelated context mutation, apply watchdog and retry rules, and stop child processes cleanly.

| ID | Requirement | Status | Evidence |

| R-139 | pi-review shall set NANA_HANDOFF=off in every child env. | untested | Dedicated role test retired by small-cuts: no recorded failure or safety line. |
| R-1683 | WHERE the platform is win32 and pi is an npm global install, pi-review shall start its pi child with NANA_HANDOFF=off in that child's environment. | untested | Dedicated role test retired by small-cuts: no recorded failure or safety line. |
| R-700 | pi-review shall run the review call under a liveness watchdog that kills the whole process group when CPU time stays flat for --stall-secs and retries with a fresh session. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/review-ledger-launch.test.mjs::stalled attempt → exit 1 (watchdog killed it)`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/review-ledger.test.mjs::after SIGKILL + a dead-pid lock, the next review is admitted` |
| R-246 | WHEN the watchdog kills a stalled review call, pi-review shall retry it with a fresh session. | untested | split from R-700 2026-10-02 (clause audit): no test pins this clause |
| R-919 | After reading captured child output, pi-watchdog shall remove its per-attempt capture directory on success, failure and stall. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/review-ledger-launch.test.mjs::pi-watchdog removes capture directories after success, child failure and stall` |
| R-1390 | WHEN pi exits 0 with review-shaped output whose recorded verdict is not empty (R-1010) before the watchdog's first poll, pi-review shall write the review to --out and exit 0 less than half a --poll interval after pi exits. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/watchdog-exit.test.mjs::R-1390: pi-review ends under half the poll after pi exits before the first poll` |
| R-1391 | WHEN pi exits non-zero at once on every attempt, pi-worker shall run all 1 + --retries attempts, printing each attempt line, each retry line and the FAILED line but no cpu line, and exit 1 less than half a --poll interval after it starts. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/watchdog-exit.test.mjs::R-1391: pi-worker runs every retry in under half the poll when pi fails at once` |
| R-1392 | WHEN pi exits between two polls after the watchdog has printed per-poll cpu lines, the watchdog shall end the attempt less than half a --poll interval after pi exits, printing no further cpu line. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/watchdog-exit.test.mjs::R-1392: an attempt ends under half the poll after pi exits between polls, with no extra cpu line` |
| R-1393 | IF pi is killed by a signal the watchdog did not send, THEN pi-review shall count that attempt as failed, run the next attempt, and after the last failed attempt exit 1 with its review checkout removed and its reservation released. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/watchdog-exit.test.mjs::R-1393: pi-review retries a pi killed by a signal, then removes its checkout and releases its reservation` |
| R-1394 | WHILE pi's CPU time stays flat, the watchdog shall kill it as stalled only after --stall-secs seconds have passed. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/watchdog-exit.test.mjs::R-1394: a flat-CPU pi is stalled only after --stall-secs` |

### SUB-009 · Accept and record a valid result
Purpose: Require the expected completion evidence and a valid reservation while invalidating results for changed review state.

| ID | Requirement | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-701 | A review shall count as produced only when its output contains a case-sensitive `VERDICT` word preceded on its line only by non-word characters. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/review-ledger-verdicts.test.mjs::pi-review enforces the case-sensitive verdict line boundary`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/review-ledger-verdicts.test.mjs::review-ledger enforces the case-sensitive verdict line boundary` |
| R-249 | A review shall count as produced only when the child exits 0 with non-empty output. | untested | split 2026-10-02 after sol r3 (clause audit): the cited checks do not reach this clause; no exit-zero or empty-output case is cited |
| R-717 | WHEN the tree changed during the review, the verdict shall not be recorded as valid. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/review-ledger-state.test.mjs::edit during the review → exit 1, 'changed during the review', verdict NOT recorded as valid` |
| R-838 | WHEN the tree changed during the review, the round shall be consumed for the admitted revision as unverified with the new state recorded. | untested | split from R-717, downgraded 2026-10-04 (EARS form batch A3, astra r2 gap audit §2): no record pins "for the admitted revision as unverified with the new state recorded — suppressing the entire tally append proves consumption sensitivity, not the retained record's revision or metadata." No new mutation was recorded this round (bc-method-ruling.md §4: gaps are listed, not gated — downgrade now, worklist for 6b). |
| R-718 | A completion shall own a live reservation. | untested | downgraded 2026-10-04 (EARS form batch A3, astra r2 gap audit §2): no record pins "own a live reservation — forcing mine=false rejects a legitimate completion; it does not exercise acceptance without ownership or liveness." No new mutation was recorded this round (bc-method-ruling.md §4: gaps are listed, not gated — downgrade now, worklist for 6b). |
| R-839 | An expired, consumed or forged reservation shall record nothing. | untested | split from R-718, downgraded 2026-10-04 (EARS form batch A3, astra r2 gap audit §2): no record pins "consumed or forged … record nothing — the aggregate audit-count assertion runs before those attempts; the consumed-reservation citation checks refusal, not recording." No new mutation was recorded this round (bc-method-ruling.md §4: gaps are listed, not gated — downgrade now, worklist for 6b). |
| R-969 | Each completed review shall record as its verdict the verdict word of its selected line — the final non-blank line of its output, after one leading byte-order mark is removed, when that line's canonical form carries `verdict:` not preceded by a letter, digit or underscore, otherwise its first non-blank line — where a line's canonical form has Unicode default-ignorable code points removed, configured look-alike letters folded, NFKC applied, default-ignorables removed and look-alikes folded again, then all letters lower-cased, where the final line may carry before the label only whitespace and the marks `#`, `>`, `-`, `*`, `_` and backtick, the first line must begin with `VERDICT: ` and carry no marks, and either may carry after the word one whole-number score written `(n/m)`, `— n/m` or ` n/m`, one trailing `.` (the final line also `,`, `;` or `:` and the marks `*`, `_` and backtick) and whitespace. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/review-ledger-launch.test.mjs::complete records the LAND corpus forms and final SEND-BACK`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/review-ledger-verdicts.test.mjs::complete extracts plain first-line verdicts, supported scores, BOM and line endings` (including final-line bare scores), `packages/nana-pack/tests/review-ledger-verdicts.test.mjs::complete refuses marks, corrections, conflicting verdicts, invisible characters and bounded-token lookalikes` (including post-NFKC fold cases), `packages/nana-pack/tests/review-ledger-verdicts.test.mjs::every configured verdict look-alike canonicalizes to its target` |
| R-1010 | IF the selected line is not a verdict line R-969 allows, or the final line is selected while the first non-blank line's canonical form (R-969) carries `verdict:` not preceded by a letter, digit or underscore and that first line contains a folded label LAND, SEND-BACK, FIX-FIRST, LAND-WITH-FIXES or the final label, or a word starting with BLOCK or REJECT or the phrase REQUEST CHANGES (folding U+2010–U+2015 and spaces inside SEND BACK and FIX FIRST to hyphens without joining words across a soft hyphen) but is not a plain verdict line with the same word, or the first line is selected while the canonical form of any non-blank line at a different position contains `verdict`, THEN the review ledger shall record an empty verdict for that review. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/review-ledger-launch.test.mjs::complete records an empty verdict for malformed verdict suffixes`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/review-ledger-verdicts.test.mjs::complete refuses marks, corrections, conflicting verdicts, invisible characters and bounded-token lookalikes`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/review-ledger-verdicts.test.mjs::a recorded prose Verdict opener reads the final label but folded conflicting labels still refuse` |
| R-1620 | — | retired | retired (was 371fee63) |
| R-1621 | WHEN pi-review records a completed review, it shall exit 1 without its SUCCESS line if the verdict is empty and otherwise retain its exit 0 and SUCCESS line. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/review-ledger-verdicts.test.mjs::pi-review exits 1 without its success line when the recorded verdict is empty` |
| R-1622 | WHEN a completed review's recorded verdict is empty, its launcher shall print exactly one stderr line naming the selected physical line and escaped text, the condition that emptied it, and the first conflicting line and escaped text when one exists. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/review-ledger-verdicts.test.mjs::an empty verdict prints one stderr line naming the selected line, its condition and the conflicting line` |
| R-1623 | WHEN a completed review's recorded verdict is empty, its launcher shall state that nana-land will refuse the revision for good, direct a new commit and review, and name the kept output. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/review-ledger-verdicts.test.mjs::the empty-verdict line directs a new commit and names the output without rounds-left paperwork` |

# CAP-03 · Control trust and tool actions
Purpose: Make trust decisions, action restrictions and policy changes visible and predictable.
Outcome: A developer can understand which sources and actions are allowed and can recover from a refusal without silently weakening controls.
Kind: constraint
Binds: CAP-01, CAP-02, CAP-04, CAP-05, CAP-07

## FEAT-009 · Decide which project content is trusted
Purpose: Represent explicit project trust, explain untrusted sources and avoid gaining trust from incidental files.
Status: active
Also applies: R-069, R-190
Artifact: A-010 · project trust store · user-home store: <pi agent dir>/trust.json · manual: the owner runs nana-setup trust or pi's /trust by hand; pi's own trust module writes the file

| R-2491 | WHERE the platform is win32, WHEN a `cd` or `pushd` to `/tmp` or a folder below it comes earlier in a bash command line than an rm, the gate shall judge that rm's relative targets from that folder under TMP, or TEMP when TMP is unset or empty. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/gate-rm-outside.test.mjs::on Windows a cd to Git Bash /tmp moves relative rm targets into the temp folder` |
| R-2492 | WHERE the platform is win32, the gate's Git Bash reading shall read a bash path spelling that begins with `/`, one letter and then `/` or its end as that letter's drive, upper-cased, with the rest of the path below that drive's root. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/gate-rm-outside.test.mjs::on Windows a Git Bash drive spelling such as /c/ reads as that drive's path` |
| R-2493 | IF an rm with no recursive flag and no `--force` names a target that only resembles a Git Bash temp or drive spelling (`/tmpx/y`, `/tmp2/y`, `/cd/y`), that climbs out of `/tmp` with `..` (collapsed before any link is read), that passes a link in the temp folder leading outside, or that names `/tmp/…` from pi's powershell tool or on a platform other than win32, THEN the gate shall still ask, naming the path it reads, when that path lies outside the working folder and every accepted temp folder. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/gate-rm-outside.test.mjs::Git Bash lookalikes, climb-outs, links out of /tmp, PowerShell /tmp and /tmp off Windows still ask` |
| R-2494 | WHERE the platform is win32, IF TMP, or TEMP when TMP is unset, names a drive or filesystem root, the home folder or a folder above it, THEN the gate shall still ask for a bash rm of a `/tmp/<name>` target, naming `<name>` under that folder. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/gate-rm-outside.test.mjs::a Git Bash /tmp target still asks when TMP names a drive root, the home folder or a folder above it` |
| R-2495 | WHERE the platform is win32, WHEN a headless pi session's bash tool sends `rm -f /tmp/<name>` or `cd /tmp && rm -f <name>` and TMP names a temp folder the gate's shared temp-folder rule accepts, the gate shall let it run with no headless block. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/gate-rm-outside.test.mjs::on Windows a headless rm -f of a Git Bash /tmp file runs unblocked`; Windows-leg-only |

| R-2497 | WHERE the platform is win32 and Git Bash is installed at `%ProgramFiles%\Git\bin\bash.exe`, where pi looks first, the gate's Git Bash readings shall agree with that Git Bash's own: its reading of `/<drive>/<rest>` with `cygpath -w`, over a `/tmp` mount of type `usertemp`. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/gate-rm-outside.test.mjs::on Windows the gate reads Git Bash drive spellings as Git Bash's own cygpath does, over a usertemp /tmp mount`; Windows-leg-only |

## FEAT-013 · Install and connect harness runtimes
Purpose: Install and identify the intended runtime resources, hooks, skills, commands and configuration while preserving foreign content.
Status: active
Also applies: R-004
Artifact: A-011 · user pack config · user-home store: <pi agent dir>/nana-pack.json · manual: the owner edits it by hand runs nana-roster set, or runs nana-setup install, which seeds a missing copy, all by hand
Artifact: A-012 · Claude hook links · user-home store: ~/.claude/hooks/ · manual: the owner runs nana-setup install by hand
Artifact: A-013 · Claude rule links · user-home store: ~/.claude/rules/ · manual: the owner runs nana-setup install by hand
Artifact: A-060 · Claude skill links · user-home store: ~/.claude/skills/ · manual: the owner runs nana-setup install by hand
Artifact: A-061 · Claude settings · user-home store: ~/.claude/settings.json · manual: the owner runs nana-setup install or uninstall by hand, which adds or removes only nana's hook entries; Claude Code and the owner write the rest
Artifact: A-062 · shared memory store · user-home store: ~/.claude/nana-memory/shared/ · manual: the owner runs nana-setup install by hand, which seeds a missing index; Claude Code sessions write the memory entries
Artifact: A-063 · PATH command links · user-home store: ~/.local/bin/ · manual: the owner runs nana-setup install by hand, which links each nana command there, or writes sh and .cmd shims on Windows
Artifact: A-064 · pi settings · user-home store: <pi agent dir>/settings.json · manual: the owner runs nana-setup install by hand, which runs pi install for each nana package and pi writes the entries; the owner edits the rest by hand

Artifact: A-066 · pi agent definitions · retired: setup-retire stops seeding reviewer.md and removes unchanged historical seeds
Artifact: A-081 · soul rule · tracked: packages/nana-setup/claude/rules/nana-soul.md · manual: edited by hand in reviewed lanes

### SUB-013 · Connect hooks and packages without duplication
Purpose: Recognize equivalent hook commands and package registrations while retaining unrelated settings and entries.

|---|---|---|---|

## FEAT-029 · Navigate code and investigate impact
Purpose: Maintain a generated module map, suggest precedent code for planners and find candidate affected requirements through source and test relationships.
Status: active
Also applies: G-004, G-007, G-008, G-009, G-010, G-011, G-019
Artifact: A-032 · runtime map · clone store: <git common dir>/nana/runtime-map.json · land: scripts/runtime-measure.mjs
Reads: A-009 · scripts/code-map.mjs
Reads: A-030 · scripts/code-map.mjs
Note (moved from old §13c on 2026-10-09):
Not a nana-pack module: the generator both project templates ship
(`templates/typescript/template/scripts/code-map.mjs`, `templates/python/template/scripts/code_map.py`),
which G-009/G-010/G-011 already cover generically. nana-pi exercises the TypeScript one
directly, over its own repo, through the shim at `scripts/code-map.mjs` AND, for R-860's full
breadth, by importing the template module itself (tests may import anything, and `templates/`
is not a mapped root — G-007); the Python one has no shim (nana-pi carries no Python source),
so it is exercised only through a rendered project in
`packages/nana-pack/tests/templates-render.test.mjs`. Added 2026-10-05 (map-test-links lane,
the owner's call: "Fix the map" — `research/karpathy-x-2026-10-04.md` background) after measuring
that the TypeScript generator's dynamic-import detection only matched a bare string literal
argument to `import(...)`, never nana-pi's own tests' `await import(new URL("../lib/x.mjs",
import.meta.url).href)` form — 67 of this repo's 95 test modules linked to no mapped module as
a result, and 56 of 77 non-test modules had no direct test caller the map could show. Two
filters were tried after that fix and BOTH retired, in order: R-863 (astra r1 MUST 1: a
comment/string-aware lexer gating all four patterns) lost real edges in ordinary code
(division misread after a completed template or object literal, a `//` comment ended by CR
or U+2028) while still fabricating edges from valid multi-line strings — astra r2,
`docs/reviews/map-test-links-2026-10-05/astra-r2.md`, 2026-10-05. R-864 (that round's own
landing call: a narrower guard dropping only a `//`-line or `*`-line match) ALSO lost real
edges — a generator method's yielded import, a leading `*` multiplying an awaited import's
property, and a real import whose physical line happened to start with `//` because a
multi-line string or a backslash continuation put it there — astra r3,
`docs/reviews/map-test-links-2026-10-05/astra-r3.md`, 2026-10-05. Both filters failed for the
same reason: a regex over raw text cannot reliably tell code from non-code without becoming a
parser, and a half-parser is worse than none, because it can LOSE a real edge instead of
merely adding a spurious one. The seat's final call (astra r3's landing recommendation,
2026-10-05): subtract filtering entirely. The four patterns match raw source exactly as on
`main`, plus DYNAMIC_URL's whitespace tolerance (R-860). The accepted residual — a spurious
edge from text in a string, a template literal or a comment — is recorded in Open questions,
not hidden behind a heuristic that risks the opposite, worse failure. IDs consumed from the
pack's own continuation block (R-756 to R-879), not a new one — template rows already live
there (R-737, R-738, R-756, R-858, R-859); R-863's and R-864's ids are never reused.

Historical measurement, preserved at the 2026-11-09 cut: on nana-pi itself after the fix (R-860): 245 import edges over the same 172 modules
(144 before), 0 new `--check` problems; 27 of 77 non-test modules now have no direct test
caller the map can show (56 before); 13 of 95 test modules still import no mapped module
(`--impact` then named them — `apps/desk/test/host-rule.test.mjs`,
`apps/desk/test/origin-rule.test.mjs`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/agent-dir-hostile.test.mjs`,
`packages/nana-pack/tests/config-gate-fallback.test.mjs`,
`packages/nana-pack/tests/config-handlers-malformed.test.mjs`,
`packages/nana-pack/tests/config-project-gate-fallback.test.mjs`,
`packages/nana-pack/tests/review-ledger.test.mjs`,
`packages/nana-pack/tests/templates-render.test.mjs`,
`packages/nana-pack/tests/test-runner.test.mjs`,
`packages/nana-setup/tests/agent-dir-consumers.test.mjs`,
`packages/nana-setup/tests/project-dismiss.test.mjs`,
`packages/nana-setup/tests/project.test.mjs`,
`packages/nana-setup/tests/relative-agent-dir.test.mjs` — each either only spawns a child
process or builds the imported path from a template string, the two forms R-860 does not
cover).

| R-626 | A full suite run shall hold the machine-wide suite lock for its whole duration, release it on every handled exit, and reclaim a lock with a missing, empty, whitespace-only, or non-decimal pid file only when it is older than 60 seconds; simultaneous reclamation attempts on the same dead lock may both proceed, causing shared CPU use but no data loss. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/test-locked.test.mjs::two full suite commands do not overlap`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/test-locked.test.mjs::handled signals release the full-run lock`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/test-locked.test.mjs::a nonzero suite exit is preserved and releases the lock`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/test-locked.test.mjs::pid initialization failure removes only the newly created lock`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/test-locked.test.mjs::handled signals wait for suite termination before releasing the lock`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/test-locked.test.mjs::the default lock lives under the temporary home`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/test-locked.test.mjs::a dead holder lock is reclaimed`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/test-locked.test.mjs::a pid-less lock older than one minute is reclaimed`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/test-locked.test.mjs::an expired whitespace-only pid lock is reclaimed`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/test-locked.test.mjs::a fresh empty-pid lock remains protected` |

## FEAT-033 · Adopt explicit engineering standards
Purpose: Keep shared configuration and module-boundary policies in one place and link their applicability to project features.
Status: active
Also applies: R-756, R-2660, R-2662
Artifact: A-037 · standards rule · tracked: packages/nana-setup/claude/rules/nana-standards.md · manual: edited by hand in reviewed lanes
Artifact: A-038 · shared general rows · tracked: templates/_shared/requirements-general.md · manual: edited by hand in reviewed lanes
Artifact: A-072 · code map · retired: gen-files lane generates docs/code-map.md locally with npm run map instead of tracking it; map:check validates sources and map:search renders in memory

| ID | Requirement | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-3870 | WHEN template acceptance runs on Windows, it shall resolve commands on PATH and launch cmd/bat shims through the shared safe Windows launcher while preserving direct executable and non-Windows launches. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/template-acceptance.test.mjs::Windows acceptance resolves shims safely and leaves direct launches unchanged` |
| R-3871 | WHEN an acceptance spawn result contains an error, its output text shall retain stdout and stderr and append the error code and message. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/template-acceptance.test.mjs::spawn error output appends its code and message without losing output` |
Note (moved from old §Part G on 2026-10-09):
These rows are the same in every nana project and are **never renumbered or edited
per project** — nana-pi is not copier-tracked; its cells mirror the shared file under
R-756. In rendered projects, the file is refreshed by `uvx copier update`. Status and
Evidence remain project-owned in REQUIREMENTS.md.

Where a row says *every module*, that is every module under the roots declared in
`code-map.config.json`: the packages' `lib/`, `bin/` and `extensions/`, the bench and
this repo's own `scripts/`, plus the test roots, each declared `layerExempt` so a test may
import across the layer direction — not declared outside the map. The recorded study trees
and `templates/` (whose headers name the RENDERED project's paths) are declared outside the
map by `ignore`; three bench modules content-pinned by a closed study are
declared `exempt` with their reasons. Generated and checked by `npm run map` /
`npm run map:check` — shims over the generator the templates ship.

# Open questions and deliberate omissions

## Open questions

- (pi 1.0 lane, 2026-10-04) mcp.json's own shape failures (not JSON, not an object, mcpServers not an object) are exercised by three uncited checks in packages/nana-setup/tests/doctor-detail.test.mjs but have no row. Add an R-366-shaped row, or declare them R-365's error handling.
- (writing trial, astra r3, 2026-10-04) R-753/R-755's cap is a CHARACTER budget on the injected block, while the read ceiling (R-755) is a BYTE budget on the file. A valid multi-byte-heavy rule (e.g. a 2,000-character Chinese file) can be cut well short of 4,000 characters while the notice still reads "cut at 4000 chars" — the notice is imprecise in that direction. Document the distinction; the byte/character gap is accepted, not fixed, for the trial.

1. The pack's README is 721 lines of declared behaviour and produced 258 rows; the extraction notes name a safe trim (merge same-status rows inside the gate, handoff and pi-review sections). Trim, or keep the grain?
6b. **Clause-level honesty.** The form half closed 2026-10-04 (`docs/reviews/ears-form-2026-10-04/lane-close-ruling.md`); the coverage half is this question. Origin: sol r3 2026-10-02 showed the rail proves marker identity and citation existence, not clause coverage; a 25-row sample pinned fully in 16 (64%). The EARS-form lane then split every multi-`shall` row: 200 origins into 244 new rows, 0 off form, G-013 `implemented`. `EARS_ALLOWANCE` sits at 0, sealed and held equal to the measured count (R-757). The lane judged the rows it touched under a standard that tightened as it ran. A3 and B's `implemented` rows (10 and 10 split-born, 9 and 7 origins) carry one recorded red mutation per named condition. In those, astra read every record and replayed one in five (`bc-method-ruling.md`). A1 and A2's (32 and 30 split-born, 33 and 28 origins) carry one red mutation per row, sampled 20 per batch at 19 of 20. C's 44 origins and the 289 implemented rows never off form carry their pre-lane cites, unjudged. Under the per-condition standard the worker-claimed rows had an uncovered condition in 27 of 46 (A3) and 66 of 83 (B). Each now reads `untested` with the uncovered words in its cell. The lane left 214 rows `untested`: 161 split-born and 53 downgraded origins. By batch: 7, 5, 9, 34, 90 and 69 for batch 0, A1, A2, A3, B and C. It ran no closing sample (`lane-close-ruling.md` §3). Worklist, in order. (1) The 214 untested rows, read from their cells: A3 and B's quote the uncovered words, the rest carry a batch sentence. (2) The re-judge tier: A1 and A2's 62 implemented split-born rows and 61 origins, then C's 44 origins, then the 289. Method is open. Per-condition records did not converge from the worker side (58.7% then 79.5% gap); the reviewer's text read found every gap the mutation hunt found. The next design starts there. Until a cell names a per-condition record, an implemented row means: one mutation turns one cited check red; read the cell.
7. (ears-form batch 0, astra r1 NOTE, 2026-10-04; RULED by astra r2, 2026-10-04) R-757 is a DEVIATION: design-ruling.md §1 only requires the rail to fail when the off-form count *exceeds* the allowance (a ceiling), so a count that quietly drops below the allowance — stale headroom — was never caught; the allowance only ever tightens by a human editing it at landing. R-757 adds a nana-pi-only assertion that the measured count EQUALS `EARS_ALLOWANCE`, failing loudly on any drift either direction. A fresh scaffold needs no equivalent row, because both its measured count and its default allowance are 0. **Ruling (astra r2): keep R-757 as a nana-pi-only repository-suite invariant.** It supplements the ceiling — it does not auto-lower the allowance, and it does not stop one old off-form row being exchanged for a new one. Generic `check()` and both templates' behaviour stay the ceiling-only `count > allowance`, unchanged; the enforcement is additive and lives only in `packages/nana-pack/tests/requirements-trace.test.mjs`, never in the reusable rail. Closed at landing (Fable, batch0-land-ruling.md §2): kept. Any commit that moves the off-form count moves `EARS_ALLOWANCE` and its seal in the same commit.
8. (ears-form batch 0, astra r2 RESIDUALS to record, 2026-10-04) (a) The form check (G-013) counts `shall` tokens; it does not establish that a row makes one semantic promise, nor that its clause has test coverage — form compliance is orthogonal to the clause-level honesty residual already tracked at 6b. (b) Generic `check()`/template allowances remain ceilings only (`count > allowance`); R-757's equality enforcement is a nana-pi-only suite addition, not a change to the reusable rail's or either template's semantics. (c) The rendered language suites run through the template-acceptance gate at release and in root CI, not in `npm test`. (d) This worktree's missing root `node_modules` and `apps/bench/.ext` mean a green full-repository `npm test` cannot be claimed while those are absent — only the EARS-touched tests are claimed green here; `packages/nana-pack/tests/readme-check.test.mjs`'s 5 environmental problems are pre-existing and unrelated to this lane.
10. (map-test-links lane, 2026-10-05) R-860 only teaches the TypeScript code-map generator the `new URL(<literal>, import.meta.url)` dynamic-import form. A dynamic import whose argument is NOT a string literal — built from a template string, a variable, or any other expression — still resolves to no edge, SILENTLY: the generator records no problem for it, unlike the Python generator, which fails `--check` naming it `unmapped dynamic import at line N`. Measured on nana-pi (2026-10-05): about 56 such sites exist in this repo alone (pi's own entry point, a handful of temp-file loaders, code built inside a spawned child-process script's own source string) — making the TypeScript generator fail on them the way Python's does would turn every TypeScript project (nana-pi included) red on day one. Not changed in this lane. The owner's call later: fail the same way Python does (and fix the ~56 sites), or document the asymmetry and leave it. In a `.ts` project a bare `import(new URL(…))` is a type error, so users tend to write `String(new URL(…))`, `new URL(…).toString()` or `fileURLToPath(new URL(…))` instead; all three are in this silent class (land ruling, 2026-10-05).
11. (map-test-links lane, 2026-10-05) A separate, pre-existing gap found while measuring R-862 on a fresh Python scaffold: the Python generator's import graph does not resolve a bare `import code_map` (or `import readme_check`, `import requirements_trace`) the way `tests/test_code_map.py`, `tests/test_readme_check.py` and `tests/test_requirements_trace.py` actually import the script they exercise (via a `sys.path` insert, not a dotted `scripts.code_map` import) — so those three test modules, plus `tests/conftest.py`, show zero callees in a fresh scaffold (4 of 5 test modules `untraced tests` reports there, against 1 of 5 for the equivalent TypeScript scaffold). This is unrelated to dynamic imports (item 10) and to R-860/861/862, which only claim the PRINTED count is correct, not that the underlying graph is complete — not measured or fixed in this lane. The owner's call later: teach `dotted_name`/`_link_imports` to also recognize a bare top-level name that matches a mapped script's own basename, or leave it as a known Python-generator blind spot.
12. (map-test-links lane, 2026-10-05; rewritten round 4 after the SECOND filter tried here
    was also subtracted — astra r3, `docs/reviews/map-test-links-2026-10-05/astra-r3.md`)
    The four import-detection patterns (STATIC_FROM, BARE_IMPORT, DYNAMIC, DYNAMIC_URL)
    match raw source text, exactly as on `main` before this lane, with no filter standing
    between a match and an edge. Raw matching can create a SPURIOUS edge from text that
    merely looks like an import but is not executable: inside a single- or double-quoted
    string, inside a template literal, or inside a `//` or `/* ... */` comment — for all
    four patterns. A spurious edge is not a safe direction to fail in: it can SILENTLY
    suppress the untraced-test count `--impact` prints (a real gap in coverage now reads as
    covered), or it can make `--check` fail, loudly, naming a module that does not exist.
    Two filters were tried here, in order, and both subtracted before landing, because each
    one LOST a real import instead — a strictly worse failure than the spurious edge either
    was built to remove: R-863 (astra r1's comment/string-aware lexer, retired by astra r2)
    and R-864 (astra r2's narrower comment-line guard, retired by astra r3). Neither
    replacement is attempted a third time in this lane; distinguishing real code from a
    spurious-looking specifier without a real parser kept costing more than it bought. The
    Python generator carries no equivalent class of gap: its reader walks a real AST, so a
    comment or a string is never ambiguous with code by construction, not by a pattern the
    TypeScript side has to guess at. The owner's call later: build a real parser (a materially
    bigger lift, and a new dependency this generator is deliberately without today), or
    accept the gap as documented.
    Remedy today, when a spurious edge fails `--check` naming a module that does not exist:
    add that path to the `ignore` list in `code-map.config.json`, as nana-pi already does
    for three paths that exist only as text inside a test (land ruling, 2026-10-05).

13. (2026-10-09 requirements audit, C03; verified in `docs/reviews/requirements-proposal-2026-10-09/`) The status legend at the top of this file says `untested` means "the code does it, nothing pins it" and `implemented` means "a test in this repo asserts it". At the audit, 75 of the 264 `untested` rows named unpinned words in their cell (a partial pin), 111 carried a batch sentence and 49 read `—`; 6b says an `implemented` row means one mutation turns one cited check red. Proposed: reword this file's legend to match. The requirements skill and both templates carry the same legend and change with 6b's ruling, through a template release. Whose call: the seat for this file; the owner for the templates, with 6b.
14. R-739's half closed on 2026-10-20. (2026-10-09 requirements audit, C04; verified in `docs/reviews/requirements-proposal-2026-10-09/`) R-739 repeats R-302 (the requirements skill's install link), and R-834 repeats R-972 (any number of reviews on one revision is one round). The check that pins a repeated review under one role carries no marker. Proposed: retire R-739 as a duplicate of R-302; mark that check with R-972, then retire R-834 as superseded by R-972. Whose call: the seat.
15. (2026-10-09 requirements audit, C05; verified in `docs/reviews/requirements-proposal-2026-10-09/`) A template update can rewrite a G row's governing text in REQUIREMENTS-general.md (R-578) while the project's status for that row stays as judged against the old text; the drift only prints a line (R-583). Should a template rewrite of a G row count as a rewrite after judgement, sending the row back to be judged? Whose call: the owner, with 6b.
16. (2026-10-09 requirements audit, C06; verified in `docs/reviews/requirements-proposal-2026-10-09/`) R-778 ("A record on the nearest recorded ancestor shall grant trust") lost its affirmative sense when it was split from R-075: read alone, a recorded decline on the ancestor would grant trust. R-028 says a store owned by this user counts as vouched, though ownership passes only the ownership check. R-075 still carries the subfolder clause that R-242 now owns. Proposed: R-778 "a recorded true on the nearest recorded ancestor, with no nearer record, shall count as a decided trust"; R-028 "ownership by this user passes only the ownership check"; drop the subfolder clause from R-075. The cited tests already fail each wrong reading. Whose call: the seat.
17. (2026-10-09 requirements audit, C07; verified in `docs/reviews/requirements-proposal-2026-10-09/`) R-764 allows benign commands that merely mention a dangerous form, but R-040 gates rm text anywhere in a segment, and interpreter deletion text is scanned across the whole command. Read alone, R-764 invites reviving the rm-text relaxation retired as R-632 and R-637 on 2026-10-07. Proposed: name both exceptions in R-764 and leave the matcher unchanged. Whose call: the seat.
18. (2026-10-09 requirements audit, C09; verified in `docs/reviews/requirements-proposal-2026-10-09/`) R-937's half closed on 2026-10-12. R-938 has no actor ("a stale copy shall read ✗ naming the file and be refreshed"): doctor reads ✗ and install refreshes. R-980, R-964 and R-311 promise PATH links with no platform clause, but on Windows no pack command is linked on PATH. Proposed: give R-938 doctor as its actor and move the refresh to an install row; open R-980, R-964 and R-311 with "WHERE the platform is not win32," as R-953 does. Whose call: the seat.
19. (2026-10-09 requirements audit, C13; verified in `docs/reviews/requirements-proposal-2026-10-09/`) Four rows state a known limit as a `shall`: R-056 (a path the shell computes at run time shall not be caught), which R-053's four caught spellings contradict; R-731 (ledger storage shall not be bounded), which R-721 and R-722 already cover; R-625 (redirect detection shall not detect logs inside submodule contents); and R-771. A requirements-first agent could refuse an improvement as a violation. Proposed: restate R-056 and R-625 as declared non-guarantees, or retire them into the pack README's limits; retire R-731 as covered by R-721 and R-722; keep R-771's meaning. Whose call: the seat.
20. (2026-10-09 requirements audit, C14; verified in `docs/reviews/requirements-proposal-2026-10-09/`) G-011 says a change's transitive callers and callees come from the map by one command. Its one cited check pins callers only, and its cell does not point to the graph's known gaps in 10–12. Proposed: name the unpinned callees clause and 10–12 in G-011's evidence cell. Whose call: the seat.
21. (2026-10-09 requirements audit, C15; verified in `docs/reviews/requirements-proposal-2026-10-09/`) Eight desk rows (R-413, R-418, R-421, R-426, R-427, R-428, R-434, R-454) read `—` as evidence, though the 2026-10-09 verification found that hand-run browser suites under `apps/desk/test/` cover them; 6 records that those suites sit outside the suite of record. Proposed: name the suite in each cell when the desk reopens; the desk is frozen until then. Whose call: the seat, after the freeze.
22. (2026-10-09 requirements audit, C16; verified in `docs/reviews/requirements-proposal-2026-10-09/`) Its stale-question half closed 3. R-865 accepts the legacy receipts config keys "during the compatibility release", with no end. Proposed: an end condition (a pack version past 0.4.0, or a date) mirrored in the config loader and the pack README. Whose call: the seat.
23. ## Deliberate omissions

- Study data under `apps/bench/studies/**` is evidence, never a requirement.
- The pack gate is advisory by doctrine (a load-path convenience, not a security boundary); no row claims otherwise.
- Nothing in the catch ledger E1 may change roles, models or spend (HANDOFF); rows record the posture, not a licence.
